0.0;\n    T c = 0.0;\n    for (const auto& val : data) {\n        T y = val - c;\n        T t = sum + y;\n        c = (t - sum) - y;\n        sum = t;\n    }\n    return sum;\n}\n\n// Print results gFirst + 1);\n        const size_t offset = (size_t)(gFirst - (gz0 - 1)) * plane;\n        const size_t total = plane * (size_t)nInit;\n        const int threads = 256;\n        const int blocks = (int)std::min<size_t>((total + threads - 1) / threads, 65535 * 16);\n        if (total > 0) {\n            initKernel<<<blocks, threads>>>(d_a + offset, nx, ny, gFirst, nInit);\n 0, comm, &reqs[nreq++]);\n                if (hasLo) MPI_Isend(sendLo, 1, planeType, lower, 0, comm, &reqs[nreq++]);\n                if (hasHi) MPI_Isend(sendHi, 1, planeType, upper, 1, comm, CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n    }\n\n    MPI_Barrier(MPI_COMM_WORLD);\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = No NaN or Inf values\n    int bad = 0;\n    #pragma omp parallel for reduction(|:bad) schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        if (std::isnan(g[i]) || std::isinf(g[i])) bad = 1;\n    }\n    if (bad) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // 2. Values should be reasonable (bounded)\n    Real minVal = grid[0];\n    Real maxVal = grid[0];\n    #pragma omp parallel for reduction(min:minVal) reduction(max:maxVal) schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, g[i]);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, g[i]);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Value range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n\n    // After averaging, values should be somewhat bounded\n    if (maxVal > 1e6 || minVal < -1e6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // 3. bool active = worldRank < nActive;\n    MPI_Comm comm;\n    MPI_Comm_split(MPI_COMM_WORLD, active ? 0 : MPI_UNDEFINED, worldRank, &comm);\n\n    // Plane counts / offsets for all active ranks (needed by root for gathering)\n    std::vector<int> planeCounts(nActive), planeOffsets(nActive);\n    for (int r = 0; r < nActive; ++r) {\n        const size_t base = nz / nActive, rem = nz % nActive;\n        planeCounts[r] = (int)(base + ((size_t)r < rem ? 1 : 0));\n        planeOffsets[r] = (int)(r * base + std::min<size_t>((size_t)r, rem));\n    }\n\n    // ---- GPU selection: round-robin over node-local ranks ----\n    MPI_Comm nodeComm;\n    MPI_Comm_split_type(MPI_COMM_WORLD, MPI_COMM_TYPE_SHARED, worldRank, MPI_INFO_NULL, &nodeComm);\n    int nodeRank = 0;\n    MPI_Comm_rank(nodeComm, MPI_Abort(MPI_COMM_WORLD, 1);\n    }\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaSetDevice(nodeRank % nDev));\n\n    int rank = 0, nRanks = 1, nl = 0;\n    long gz0 = 0;          // global z of local plane 1 (first owned CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n        }\n        // Boundary values are copied unchanged every iteration, so both buffers\n        // start out identical and only interior points are ever MPI_PROC_NULL);\n        const bool hasHi = (upper != MPI_PROC_NULL);\n\n        // Boundary planes (adjacent to halos) and interior planes of this slab\n        const bool firstIsInt = (zIntLo <= 1         if (firstIsInt) launchStencil(in, out, nx, ny, 1, 1, sBnd);\n            if (lastIsInt) launchStencil(in, out, nx, ny, nl, nl, sBnd);\n            launchStencil(in, out, nx, ny, innerLo, CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n    }\n\n    MPI_Barrier(MPI_COMM_WORLD);\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n\n    if (root) {\n        printf(\"Computation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n\n        // Calculate performance metrics\n        double cellUpdates = (double)((nx-2) * (ny-2) * (nz-2)) * iterations;\n        double mcups = cellUpdates / (duration.count() / 1000.0) / 1e6;  // Million cell updates per second\n        printf(\"Performance: %.3f MCellUpdates/s\\n\", mcups);\n    }\n\n    int exitCode = 0;\n    if (printResults || validate) {\n        // Gather the final grid on the root rank\n        std::vector<Real> finalGrid;\n        if (active) {\n            const Real* d_final = (iterations % 2 == 0) ? d_a : d_b;\n            if (root) {\n                finalGrid.resize(gridSize);\n ny, nz);\n\n                if (valid) {\n                    printf(\"Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n                } else {\n                    printf(\"Validation: FAILED\\n\");\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfjQXCgGYpa1TL2oBudEn","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've parallelized stencil3d with MPI, CUDA and OpenMP, changing only `stencil3d.cpp` and `CMakeLists.txt`; the executable is still `stencil3d`. On this 4× RTX 3090 node, a 512³ grid runs at about 88 billion cell updates/s on 4 GPUs. The original serial code ran at about 0.41 billion/s, measured on a 256³ grid. Results are bit-for-bit identical to the original: the printed checksums, samples and hash matched in all 40 test runs (8 grid sizes and iteration counts, each with 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 ranks). That includes tiny grids and runs with more ranks than z-planes. Validation passes, and a clean build has no warnings.\n\n| Ranks (GPUs), 512³ | Billion cell updates/s |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | ~28 |\n| 2 | ~55 |\n| 4 | ~88 |\n\nTimings vary by about ±10% between runs. The 3-rank case was only measured once, before the last two speed-ups, so I've left it out.\n\n**How it's split up**\n- **MPI:** the grid is cut into slabs along z, one per rank, each with a one-plane halo on either side. If there are more ranks than planes, the extra ranks sit idle. At the end, the full grid is gathered on rank 0 only when `-r` or `-v` is given.\n- **CUDA:** each rank uses a GPU on its node and keeps both grid buffers on the GPU for the whole run. Each iteration first computes the two planes next to the halos on a high-priority stream. Those planes are then exchanged with the neighbours while the rest of the slab is computed on a second stream.\n- **OpenMP:** used for the min/max and NaN/Inf checks during validation on rank 0.\n- **Boundary handling:** the original copies boundary values unchanged every iteration. Instead, both buffers start with the initial values and only interior points are ever rewritten, which gives the same result with less memory traffic.\n\n**Changes you might not expect**\n- **Division by 7:** double-precision division is very slow on these consumer GPUs and was the main bottleneck. I replaced `/7.0` with a multiply by 1/7 plus one fused multiply-add correction. A standard result (Markstein's theorem) says this gives exactly the same rounded answer, and a GPU test of 1.7×10¹¹ values found no differences. The order of the additions is kept exactly as in the original.\n- **Halo exchange goes through host memory:** the installed Open MPI can't send GPU memory directly, so halo planes are copied to pinned host buffers and back. Those buffers are allocated on the memory node next to each rank's GPU, which roughly doubled the speed of those copies.\n- **CPU binding is untouched:** I tried pinning each rank permanently to its GPU's CPU socket, but it made MPI transfers slower, so I removed it. The program only switches CPUs briefly while allocating those buffers, then restores the launcher's binding.\n\n**CMake:** the source is compiled as CUDA, and it now requires MPI, OpenMP and the CUDA toolkit. The GPU architecture defaults to whatever card is in the build machine, which you can override with `CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES`. Run it as usual with `mpirun -np <ranks> ./stencil3d ...`.\n\n**Remaining limit:** 4 GPUs reach about 3.1× one GPU, because exchanging 2 MB planes through host memory is still partly exposed. On this 4× RTX 3090 node, a 512³ grid runs at about 88 billion cell updates/s on 4 GPUs. The original serial code ran at about 0.41 billion/s, measured on a 256³ grid. Results are bit-for-bit identical to the original: the printed checksums, samples and hash matched in all 40 test runs (8 grid sizes and iteration counts, each with 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 ranks). That includes tiny grids and runs with more ranks than z-planes. Validation passes, and a clean build has no warnings.\n\n| Ranks (GPUs), 512³ | Billion cell updates/s |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | ~28 |\n| 2 | ~55 |\n| 4 | ~88 |\n\nTimings vary by about ±10% between runs. The 3-rank case was only measured once, before the last two speed-ups, so I've left it out.\n\n**How it's split up**\n- **MPI:** the grid is cut into slabs along z, one per rank, each with a one-plane halo on either side. If there are more ranks than planes, the extra ranks sit idle. At the end, the full grid is gathered on rank 0 only when `-r` or `-v` is given.\n- **CUDA:** each rank uses a GPU on its node and keeps both grid buffers on the GPU for the whole run. Each iteration first computes the two planes next to the halos on a high-priority stream. Those planes are then exchanged with the neighbours while the rest of the slab is computed on a second stream.\n- **OpenMP:** used for the min/max and NaN/Inf checks during validation on rank 0.\n- **Boundary handling:** the original copies boundary values unchanged every iteration. Instead, both buffers start with the initial values and only interior points are ever rewritten, which gives the same result with less memory traffic.\n\n**Changes you might not expect**\n- **Division by 7:** double-precision division is very slow on these consumer GPUs and was the main bottleneck. I replaced `/7.0` with a multiply by 1/7 plus one fused multiply-add correction. A standard result (Markstein's theorem) says this gives exactly the same rounded answer, and a GPU test of 1.7×10¹¹ values found no differences. The order of the additions is kept exactly as in the original.\n- **Halo exchange goes through host memory:** the installed Open MPI can't send GPU memory directly, so halo planes are copied to pinned host buffers and back. Those buffers are allocated on the memory node next to each rank's GPU, which roughly doubled the speed of those copies.\n- **CPU binding is untouched:** I tried pinning each rank permanently to its GPU's CPU socket, but it made MPI transfers slower, so I removed it. The program only switches CPUs briefly while allocating those buffers, then restores the launcher's binding.\n\n**CMake:** the source is compiled as CUDA, and it now requires MPI, OpenMP and the CUDA toolkit. The GPU architecture defaults to whatever card is in the build machine, which you can override with `CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES`. Run it as usual with `mpirun -np <ranks> ./stencil3d ...`.\n\n**Remaining limit:** 4 GPUs reach about 3.1× one GPU, because exchanging 2 MB planes through host memory is still partly exposed.
